On Linux `src/gen/genmod.py` matches. So on Windows it's NOT ignored → checked. {"type":"message_end","message":{"role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"The fix in 2.15.0 is in `_is_ignored_file`:\n\n```python\ndef _is_ignored_file(\n    element: str,\n    ignore_list: list[str],\n    ignore_list_re: list[Pattern[str]],\n    ignore_list_paths_re: list[Pattern[str]],\n) -> bool:\n    element = os.path.normpath(element)\n    basename = os.path.basename(element)\n    return (\n        basename in ignore_list\n        or _is_in_ignore_list_re(basename, ignore_list_re)\n        or _is_in_ignore_list_re(element, ignore_list_paths_re)\n    )\n```\n\nThe `element = os.path.normpath(element)` normalizes backslashes to forward slashes on Windows, so regexes written with forward slashes match.\n\nBut wait — that's only part of the story. `os.path.normpath` on Windows converts `\\` to `/`? No! `os.path.normpath` on Windows converts `/` to `\\` (the opposite). Let me think:\n\n- On Windows, `os.path.normpath(\"src/gen/about.py\")` → `src\\gen\\about.py` (converts forward slashes to backslashes).\n- On Windows, `os.path.normpath(\"src\\\\gen\\\\about.py\")` → `src\\gen\\about.py` (unchanged).\n\nHmm, so normpath on Windows actually converts `/` to `\\`. Let me summarize:\n\n## Root cause\nWhen running pylint recursively (`--recursive=y`), file/directory paths discovered by `os.walk` are compared against `ignore-paths` regexes **before normalization**. E.g., `os.walk(\".\")` yields paths like `./ignored_subdirectory/failing.py` (or on Windows, mixed/backslash separators like `src/gen\\about.py` or `src\\gen\\...`), so a regex like `^ignored_subdirectory/.*` or `^src/gen/.*$` fails to match due to the `./` prefix / path separator differences. As a result, `ignore-paths` was silently ignored in recursive mode.\n\n## Fix (matching upstream PR pylint-dev/pylint#7080, closes #6964)\n1. `pylint/lint/expand_modules.py`: normalize the element with `os.path.normpath()` in `_is_ignored_file` before matching against `ignore`/`ignore-patterns`/`ignore-paths`.\n2. `tests/test_self.py`: added `test_ignore_path_recursive_current_dir` regression test (running `pylint . --recursive=y --ignore-paths=^ignored_subdirectory/.*` from a directory whose ignored subdirectory contains lint errors; exits 0 when respected).\n3. `doc/whatsnew/2/2.14/full.rst`: changelog entry.\n\nVerified: the new test fails without the fix and passes with it; all expand_modules/unittest_lint tests pass; the only failing tests in test_self.py are pre-existing environment failures identical before/after my change.","thinkingSignature":"reasoning_content"},{"type":"text","text":"The fix is complete. For each root, it checks `_is_ignored_file(root, self.config.ignore, self.config.ignore_patterns, self.config.ignore_paths)`.\n\nThe problem: `_is_ignored_file` does:\n```python\ndef _is_ignored_file(\n    element: str,\n    ignore_list: list[str],\n    ignore_list_re: list[Pattern[str]],\n    ignore_list_paths_re: list[Pattern[str]],\n) -> bool:\n    basename = os.path.basename(element)\n    return (\n        basename in ignore_list\n        or _is_in_ignore_list_re(basename, ignore_list_re)\n        or _is_in_ignore_list_re(element, ignore_list_paths_re)\n    )\n```\n\nSo the paths being checked are `root` from `os.walk`. When you run `pylint --recursive=y src/`, the `os.walk` root paths would be relative like `src`, `src/gen`, etc. Wait, actually os.walk(\"src/\") gives roots \"src/\", \"src/gen\", ... Hmm, actually os.walk(\"src/\") yields \"src/\" then \"src/gen\". Let me think.\n\nActually os.walk(\"src/\") yields:\n- root=\"src/\" (or \"src\" depending on trailing slash)\n- then \"src/gen\"\n\nThe ignore-paths regex is `^src/gen/.*$`. When walking, root would be `src/gen` (without trailing slash). The regex `^src/gen/.*$` requires a trailing slash after `src/gen`. So `src/gen` (no trailing slash) doesn't match `^src/gen/.*$` because there's no `/` after `gen`.\n\nHmm wait, but the user's expected behavior is that `src\\gen\\*` should not be checked. The regex `^src/gen/.*$` with os.walk root `src/gen` wouldn't match anyway because there's no trailing slash.\n\nBut actually there's a deeper issue: the ignore-path check in `_discover_files` only checks `root`, not the individual files. Let me trace through:\n\n1. `os.walk(\"src/\")` yields roots: `src/`, `src/gen`.\n2. For root `src/`: not ignored. `__init__.py` not in files (probably), so yield all `.py` files in `src/`.\n3. For root `src/gen`: check `_is_ignored_file(\"src/gen\", ...)` with ignore_paths `^src/gen/.*$`. `basename` = \"gen\" doesn't match ignore-paths regex... Actually `_is_in_ignore_list_re(\"src/gen\", [\"^src/gen/.*$\"])` - does `^src/gen/.*$` match \"src/gen\"? No, because the regex requires `/` after \"gen\" and `.*` after that. So \"src/gen\" doesn't match. So the root isn't skipped, and files in `src/gen` are yielded.\n\nWait, but actually there might be an even subtler issue: `os.path.normpath` isn't applied here. But the regex `^src/gen/.*$` - the file paths yielded are `src/gen/about.py` which would match the regex. But since the check is only on `root` (the directory), and the regex doesn't match the directory `src/gen` (no trailing slash), the directory isn't skipped.\n\nHmm, but wait - what if the user's regex is `^src/gen/.*$` and the intent is to match everything under src/gen.
